\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=t_{1}}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=(t-1)\Delta+1}^{t\Delta}(1-\lambda)^{t_{3}-t_{1}}\leq(1-\lambda)^{(t-2)\Delta+1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(1-\lambda)^{k}\right)\\
\times\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(k+1)(1-\lambda)^{k}\right).

Putting together these estimates and using the fact that
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)(1-\lambda)^{k}<\infty
, we conclude the proof of Step 3.

Finally, combining Steps 1 through 3 and (
66
), the result follows.
∎

Lemma E.9
.

There exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda,\mu
and
p,
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\var_{\theta}[(\overline{U}_{\Delta})^{2}]\leq K\Delta^{2}N^{-2}.

We use the notation of the proof of Lemma
E.8
, except that we now denote
{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}
the set of all vectors
\bf z\in F_{1}\times F_{1}\times F_{1}\times F_{1}
satisfying the conditions of Item 7. of Lemma
3.8
, that is, without imposing the constraint that
t_{1}>t_{4}
or
t_{3}>t_{2}
. By taking
t=1
in inequality (
66
), it follows that

\var_{\theta}\left[\left(\overline{U}_{\Delta}\right)^{2}\right]\leq 4N^{-4}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{6}\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,p}}|B({\bf z})|\right).

(68)

So we need to show that the sum of covariances is at most
K\Delta^{2}N^{2}
. This is done in the 5 steps below.

Step 1.
First, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{p=1}^{2}\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,p}}|B({\bf z})|\leq K(\Delta N)^{2}.

By Item 1 and Item 2 of Lemma
3.8
, we have that

\sum_{p=1}^{2}\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,p}}|B({\bf z})|\leq|\mathcal{C}_{1,1}|+|\mathcal{C}_{1,2}|\leq\Delta N+2\Delta N(\Delta N-1)\leq 3(\Delta N)^{2},

so that the result follows with
K=3
.

Step 2.
Next, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,3}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN\Delta^{2}.

Item 3 of Lemma
3.8
, ensures that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,3}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}(1-\lambda)^{|t_{3}-t_{1}|}N^{-1}.

Since
\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}(1-\lambda)^{|t_{3}-t_{1}|}\leq\Delta^{2}
, the result follows from the previous inequality.

Step 3.
Here, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN\Delta^{2}.

We proceed very similarly as in the proof of Step 2 of Lemma
E.8
. First, observe that we can write (see Remark
3.9
),

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}|B({\bf z})|=\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}=z_{2},z_{3}\neq z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|+\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}\neq z_{2},z_{3}=z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|.

To obtain the result, we apply Item 4 of Lemma
3.8
to obtain an upper bound for each term on the right hand side of the above identity. Since both terms are treated very similarly, we explain how we deal with the first term only. By applying Point 4 of Lemma
3.8
to the first one, we obtain that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}=z_{2},z_{3}\neq z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{3}\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{|s_{3}-s_{2}|+|s_{2}-s_{1}|}N^{-2},

where
s_{1}\leq s_{2}\leq s_{3}
denotes the ordering of the triple
(t_{1},t_{3},t_{4})
. Next, we use that
|s_{3}-s_{2}|+|s_{2}-s_{1}|\geq t_{4}-t_{3}
to deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{|s_{3}-s_{2}|+|s_{2}-s_{1}|}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{t_{4}-t_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(1-\lambda)^{k}\leq\lambda^{-1}\Delta^{2}.

As a consequence, we obtain that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,4}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}=z_{2},z_{3}\neq z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN\lambda^{-1}\Delta^{2},

and the result follows.

Step 4.
In this step, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,5}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta^{2}.

We start observing that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,5}}|B({\bf z})|=\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,5}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}=z_{3},z_{2}\neq z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|+\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,5}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}\neq z_{3},z_{2}=z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|.

Hence, it suffices to provide an upper bound for each term on the right hand side of the above inequality. We will explain how we deal with the first one only. The second one can be treated similarly.
By Item 5 of Lemma
3.8
, we have that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,5}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{z_{1}=z_{3},z_{2}\neq z_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{3}\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{(t_{4}-t_{2})}N^{-1}.

Hence, by observing that

\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{(t_{4}-t_{2})}\leq\Delta^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(1-\lambda)^{k}\leq\Delta^{2}\lambda^{-1},

the result follows.

Step 5.
Finally, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta^{2}.

We start from

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}|B({\bf z})|\leq\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{4}\}}\right]|B({\bf z})|+\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}\right.\\
\left.+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}\right]|B({\bf z})|.

In the sequel, we handle the two terms on the right-hand side of the above inequality separately.

Step 5.1
Here, we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{4}\}}\right]|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta.

By Item 6 of Lemma
3.8
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{4}\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}\times\\
(1-\lambda)^{t_{2}-t_{3}}N^{-2}.

We upper bound

\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}(1-\lambda)^{t_{2}-t_{3}}\leq\Delta\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(1-\lambda)^{k}\right)^{3},

such that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta.

Proceeding similarly, we can also check that
\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta
. This concludes the proof of Step 5.1

Step 5.2
Here we show that there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
\Delta\geq 1
,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{2}\}}+{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{3}\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}\right]|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta^{2}.

From Item 7 of Lemma
3.8
, we deduce that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq K\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{(t_{4}-t_{2})+(t_{3}-t_{1})}N^{-2}.

Since

\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{3}=1}^{\Delta}\sum_{t_{4}=1}^{\Delta}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{(t_{4}-t_{2})+(t_{3}-t_{1})}\leq\Delta^{2}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\Delta-1}(1-\lambda)^{k}\right)^{2},

it then follows that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}(1-\lambda)^{(t_{1}-t_{3})+(t_{2}-t_{4})}N^{-2}\leq N^{2}\Delta^{2}\lambda^{-2}

such that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{t_{1}\leq t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{4}\}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta^{2}.

Proceeding similarly, we can also check the three other cases, concluding the proof of Step 5.2.

Combining Steps 5.1, 5.2 and 5.3, we obtain

\sum_{{\bf z}\in{\mathcal{C}_{1,6}}}|B({\bf z})|\leq KN^{2}\Delta^{2},

concluding the proof of Step 5.

By summing all the upper bounds provided by the Steps 1 through 5, we conclude the proof of Lemma
E.9
.

∎

We are now in position to handle the terms
D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T}
and
D^{N,2}_{2\Delta,T}
.

Proposition E.10
.

There exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda
such that for all
N\geq 1
,
T\geq 2
and
1\leq\Delta\leq\lfloor T/2\rfloor
, we have that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T}\right)^{2}+\left(D^{N,2}_{2\Delta,T}\right)^{2}\right]\leq K\frac{\Delta}{T}.

Clearly, the result will follow if we can show that
I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[(D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T})^{2}\right]\leq K\Delta T^{-1}
. To establish this inequality, first observe that

\displaystyle I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N^{2}}{T^{2}}\var_{\theta}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor}\left(\overline{U}_{t\Delta}-\overline{U}_{(t-1)\Delta}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N^{2}}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor}\sum_{s=1}^{\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor}\cov_{\theta}\left[\left(\overline{U}_{t\Delta}-\overline{U}_{(t-1)\Delta}\right)^{2},\left(\overline{U}_{s\Delta}-\overline{U}_{(s-1)\Delta}\right)^{2}\right].

Next, we use the stationarity of the system to conclude that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{N^{2}}{T^{2}}\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor\var_{\theta}[(\overline{U}_{\Delta})^{2}]\\
+2\frac{N^{2}}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=2}^{\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor}\left(\lfloor T/\Delta\rfloor-t+1\right)\cov_{\theta}\left[\left(\overline{U}_{\Delta}\right)^{2},\left(\overline{U}_{t\Delta}-\overline{U}_{(t-1)\Delta}\right)^{2}\right].

As a consequence of Lemma
E.8
and of the fact that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1-\lambda)^{k\Delta}<\infty
, it then follows that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(D^{N,2}_{\Delta,T}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{N^{2}}{T\Delta}\var_{\theta}[(\overline{U}_{\Delta})^{2}]+K(\Delta T)^{-1}.

Hence, using Lemma
E.9
, we establish the desired inequality and conclude the proof.
∎

E.3
Proof of Equation (
33
)

The proof relies on a decomposition of the error
|\hat{v}-v^{N}_{\infty}|
(see Inequality (
69
)). Each term of the decomposition is then controlled by one of the four Propositions
E.11
-
E.14
. Their proofs are quite similar and less involved than the proofs given in the previous subsection.

Recall that

\hat{v}=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(Z_{i,T}-\overline{Z}_{T})^{2}-\frac{N}{T^{2}}\overline{Z}_{T}+\frac{1}{T^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(Z_{i,T})^{2},

and that
\hat{m}=\overline{Z}_{T}/T
. Therefore,

|\hat{v}-v^{N}_{\infty}|\leq\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,2}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3},

(69)

where

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}-\frac{\bar{Z}_{T}}{T}\right)^{2}-(m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}})^{2}\right]-\frac{N}{T}\overline{m^{N}}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\right|,

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,2}=\frac{N}{T}\left|\hat{m}-\overline{m^{N}}\right|,\mbox{ and }\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}=\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}\right)^{2}-(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\right|.

Proposition E.11
.

There exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
T\geq 1
,

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,2}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}\right]\leq K\left(\frac{N}{T^{2}}+\frac{{N}^{1/2}}{T^{3/2}}\left(1+\sqrt{v_{\infty}^{N}}\right)\right).

First, remind that
I\!\!E_{\theta}[Z_{i,T}/T]=m_{i}^{N}
and so
I\!\!E_{\theta}[\hat{m}]=\overline{m^{N}}
. On the one hand, by Cauchy-Schwarz,
I\!\!E_{\theta}[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,2}]\leq NT^{-1}\left(\var_{\theta}(\hat{m})\right)^{1/2}\leq KN^{1/2}T^{-3/2}
by Equation (
32
).
On the other hand, by first rewriting
\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}
as (recall that
{U}_{i,T}=Z_{i,T}-Tm^{N}_{i}
),

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}=\frac{1}{T}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{U_{i,T}}{T}\right)^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{N}(m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}})\frac{U_{i,T}}{T}+2\frac{N}{T}\overline{m^{N}}\ \overline{U}_{T}\right|

and then using the triangle inequality, we obtain that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}\right]\leq\frac{1}{T^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(U_{i,T}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}}|I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|U_{i,T}\right|\right]+2\frac{N}{T^{2}}\overline{m^{N}}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|\overline{U}_{T}\right|\right].

Now, by stationarity and Lemma
3.7
,

\var_{\theta}\left(Z_{i,T}\right)=T\var_{\theta}\left(X_{i,0}\right)+2\sum_{t=1}^{T}(T-t)\cov_{\theta}(X_{i,0},X_{i,t})\\
\leq T\var_{\theta}\left(X_{i,0}\right)+KT/N\leq KT,

(70)

so that

\frac{1}{T^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(U_{i,T}\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{T^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\text{Var}_{\theta}\left(Z_{i,T}\right)\leq KNT^{-2}.

Moreover, by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and then Jensen inequality, we deduce that

\sum_{i=1}^{N}|m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}}|I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|U_{i,T}\right|\right]\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}}\right|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left(U_{i,T}\right)^{2}\right]}.

Hence, using once more Inequality (
70
) and reminding that
v_{\infty}^{N}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}}\right|^{2}
, we obtain that

\frac{2}{T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|m^{N}_{i}-\overline{m^{N}}|I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|U_{i,T}\right|\right]\leq K\frac{N^{1/2}}{T^{3/2}}\sqrt{v_{\infty}^{N}}.

Finally, by combining Jensen inequality and Lemma
E.6
, it follows that
I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|\overline{U}_{T}\right|\right]\leq\sqrt{I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|\overline{U}_{T}\right|^{2}\right]}\leq KT^{1/2}N^{-1/2}

so that

2\frac{N}{T^{2}}\overline{m^{N}}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\left|\overline{U}_{T}\right|\right]\leq KN^{1/2}T^{-3/2},

where we have also used that that
|\overline{m^{N}}|\leq 1
. Putting together the above estimates, it then follows that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,3}\right]\leq K\left(\frac{N}{T^{2}}+\frac{{N}^{1/2}}{T^{3/2}}\sqrt{v_{\infty}^{N}}\right),

and the result follows since we have already proved that
I\!\!E_{\theta}[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,2}]\leq KN^{1/2}T^{-3/2}
.

∎

We now turn to the study of
\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1}.
Using that

(a-b)^{2}-(\bar{a}-\bar{b})^{2}=(a-b)^{2}-(a-\bar{b})^{2}+(a-\bar{a})^{2}+2(a-\bar{a})(\bar{a}-\bar{b}),

we obtain that
\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1}\leq\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,1}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,3},
where

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,1}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}-\frac{\bar{Z}_{T}}{T}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}-\overline{m^{N}}\right)^{2}\right|,

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}-m_{i}^{N}\right)^{2}-\frac{N}{T}\overline{m^{N}}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\right|

and

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,3}=2\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}-m_{i}^{N})(m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}})\right|.

Furthermore, remind that
{U}_{i,t}=Z_{i,t}-tm^{N}_{i}
and so we write
\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2}=\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,1}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2},
with

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,1}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\frac{U_{i,T}}{T}\right)^{2}-I\!\!E_{\theta}\left(\left(\frac{U_{i,T}}{T}\right)^{2}\right)\right|

and

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}I\!\!E_{\theta}\left(\left(\frac{U_{i,T}}{T}\right)^{2}\right)-\frac{N}{T}\overline{m^{N}}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\right|.

Proposition E.12
.

For all
N\geq 1
and
T\geq 1
, there exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda
such that

I\!\!E_{\theta}\left[\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,1}+\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2}\right]\leq K\frac{1}{T}.

Adapting the argument used in the beginning of Proposition
E.1
, we have

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,1}=N\left(\frac{\bar{Z}_{T}}{T}-\overline{m^{N}}\right)^{2}.

Hence, Equation (
32
) implies that
I\!\!E_{\theta}\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,1}\leq K/T
.

Second, remark that
\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2}
is not random and rewrites as

\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2}=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\var_{\theta}\left(\frac{Z_{i,T}}{T}\right)-\frac{N}{T}\overline{m^{N}}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\right|.

Using Equation (
70
) and the fact that
\var_{\theta}(X_{i,0})=m_{i}^{N}-(m_{i}^{N})^{2}
we conclude that
I\!\!E_{\theta}\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,2}\leq K/T.

∎

Proposition E.13
.

For all
N\geq 1
and
T\geq 1
, there exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda
such that

I\!\!E_{\theta}|\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,3}|^{2}\leq\frac{K}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}|^{2}.

Clearly,

I\!\!E_{\theta}|\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,3}|^{2}\leq\frac{4}{T^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}|\cov_{\theta}(U_{i,T},U_{j,T})||m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}||m_{j}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}|.

But, by stationarity and using Lemma
3.7
,

\left|\cov_{\theta}(U_{i,T},U_{j,T})\right|\leq\sum_{s,t=1}^{T}\left|\cov_{\theta}(X_{i,s},X_{j,t})\right|\leq T\left|\cov_{\theta}(X_{i,0},X_{j,0})\right|+KT/N.

Moreover,

\left|\cov_{\theta}(X_{i,0},X_{j,0})\right|\leq(m^{N}_{i}-(m_{i}^{N})^{2}){{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{i=j\}}+K/N{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\{i\neq j\}},

such that all in all

I\!\!E_{\theta}|\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,3}|^{2}\\
\leq\frac{K}{T}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left[\frac{1}{N}+(m^{N}_{i}-(m_{i}^{N})^{2})\right](m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}})^{2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\neq j}|m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}||m_{j}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}|\right).

Since
m^{N}_{i}-(m_{i}^{N})^{2}\leq 1
and
|m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}||m_{j}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}|\leq(m_{i}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}})^{2}+(m_{j}^{N}-\overline{m^{N}})^{2},
the conclusion follows.
∎

Proposition E.14
.

For all
N\geq 1
and
T\geq 1
, there exists a constant
K
depending only on
\lambda
such that

I\!\!E_{\theta}|\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,1}|^{2}\leq KN/T^{2}.

We have that

I\!\!E_{\theta}|\Upsilon_{T}^{N,1,2,1}|^{2}=\frac{1}{T^{4}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\cov_{\theta}((U_{i,T})^{2},(U_{j,T})^{2})\\
=\frac{1}{T^{4}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\cov_{\theta}(Y_{i,s}Y_{i,s},Y_{j,t}Y_{j,t})+\frac{1}{T^{4}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{s\neq s^{\prime}}\sum_{t\neq t^{\prime}}\cov_{\theta}(Y_{i,s}Y_{i,s^{\prime}},Y_{j,t}Y_{j,t^{\prime}})\\
=:S_{1}+S_{2}.

(71)

By Lemma
3.8
, Item 1 and Item 3,

S_{1}\leq KN/T^{3}.
To deal with
S_{2},
we adapt the arguments of the proof of Lemma
E.9
. As before, write
{\bf z}=(z_{1},z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}),
and
B({\bf z})=\cov_{\theta}\left[Y_{z_{1}}Y_{z_{2}},Y_{z_{3}}Y_{z_{4}}\right].

This expression is symmetric with respect to
t,t^{\prime}
and also with respect to
s,s^{\prime}.
Now, introduce the set
F^{(2)}=\{(z_{1},z_{2}):z_{1}=(i,s),z_{2}=(i,s^{\prime}),i\in\{1,\dots,N\},s,s^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,T\},s\neq s^{\prime}\}
and define, for
p\in\{2,5,7\}
,
\mathcal{P}_{p,T}
as the set of all vectors
\mathbf{z}\in F^{(2)}\times F^{(2)}
satisfying the conditions of Item
p
of Lemma
3.8
. Moreover, notice that
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{P}_{5,T}\cup\mathcal{P}_{2,T}
implies that the first coordinates (denoted
i
and
j
above) of the four couples in
\mathbf{z}
are equal.

Then

S_{2}=\frac{1}{T^{4}}[\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{7,T}}B({\bf z})+\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{5,T}}B({\bf z})+\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2,T}}B({\bf z})].

Following Step 5 of the proof of Lemma
E.9
, it is easy so see that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{7,T}}B({\bf z})\leq KT^{2}.

Moreover, Lemma
3.8
, Item 5, implies that

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{5,T}}B({\bf z})\leq KT^{2}.

Finally,

\sum_{{\bf z}\in\mathcal{P}_{2,T}}B({\bf z})\leq K|\mathcal{P}_{2,T}|=KNT^{2}.

All in all we therefore obtain that

S_{2}\leq KN/T^{2},
implying the assertion.
∎

F
Proof of Proposition
4.2

This section contains a fine study of the random environment
\theta
. First, some matrix notation is introduced. Then, the asymptotics of the rows and columns of the rescaled random environment
A^{N}
are stated in Lemma
F.1
. In turn, the asymptotics of the rows and columns of the inverse matrix
Q^{N}
are stated in Lemmas
F.4
and
F.6
. Then, the proofs of these three lemmas are given in three disjoint subsections. Finally, the proof of Proposition
4.2
is given in the last subsection.

To readers interested in random environments that differ from the i.i.d. case, let us mention that the proof of Proposition
4.2
relies solely on the results of Lemmas
F.1
,
F.4
, and
F.6
in the sense that the same proof applies to any environment
\theta
for which these lemmas hold. Similarly, the proofs of Lemmas
F.4
and
F.6
depend only on Lemma
F.1
Therefore, only the proof Lemma
F.1
must be adapted. In what follows, we specify the adaptation required for the symmetric interaction case.

The main modification concerns the bound of the variance terms for which we use Bienaymé’s identity together with a control of the few non null covariance terms. The exhaustive list of modifications is: 1) Equation (
96
), 2) Equation (
97
) by proving that
\operatorname{Var}(\sum_{k\in{\cal P}_{a}}Z_{k})
and
I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{k\in{\cal P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]
are smaller than some universal constant, 3) check that the "independence argument" below Equation (
98
) still holds, 4) replace the "independence argument" by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to bound
\mu_{1,1}
, 5) Equation (
99
) is replaced by

I\!\!E\left[\left(Z_{i}(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq i}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]\leq(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})\var(Z_{i})+KN^{-2}

for some universal constant
K
.

Two technical results used throughout the proofs are stated as technical lemmas in Section
H
.

F.1
General notation

Hereafter, for any subset
S
of
[N]
, we write
1_{S}
to indicate the
N
-dimensional vector having value
1
in each coordinate belonging to
S
and value
0
in the remaining coordinates. To alleviate the notation, we will simply write
1_{N}
and
0_{N}
instead of
1_{[N]}
and
1_{\emptyset}
, respectively. For any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
\overline{v}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}
denotes the arithmetic mean of the coordinates of
v
,
\|v\|_{r}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}|v_{i}|^{r}\right)^{1/r}
where
r\in[1,\infty)
, denotes the
r
-norm of
v
and

\|v\|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq N}|v_{i}|
its
\infty
-norm.
For vectors
v,u\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, we write
v\odot u
to denote the vector whose
i-
th coordinate is
v_{i}u_{i},
for
1\leq i\leq N
. In other words,
v\odot u
is the Hadamard product between the vectors
v
and
u
.
To shorten the notation, we will simply note
v^{2}
instead of
v\odot v
.
Observe that, with this notation, the orthogonal projection of a vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
on the coordinates in
S\subseteq[N]
can be written as
1_{S}\odot v
. In particular, all coordinates in
S^{c}
of the vector
1_{S}\odot v
are null. One can always write
v=1_{S}\odot v+1_{S^{c}}\odot v.

For any
N
-by-
N
matrix
B
with real entries, we denote
B^{\intercal}
its transpose. For all
r\in[1,\infty]
, we denote
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}
the operator norm of
B
associated to the
r
-norm
\|\cdot\|_{r}:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:v\neq 0_{N}}\frac{\|Bv\|_{r}}{\|v\|_{r}}.

It is well-known that
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1}
and
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}
may be defined alternatively as

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1}=\max_{1\leq j\leq N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|B(i,j)|\ \text{and}\ |\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|B(i,j)|.

The following fact will also be used in the sequel: for each
r\in(1,\infty)
, it holds that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}\leq|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{1/r}_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|B|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{1-1/r}_{\infty}.

(72)

F.2
Study of the rescaled random environment

Recall that
A^{N}=(A^{N}(i,j))_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
is a rescaled version of the random environment
\theta
defined as, for each
1\leq i\leq N
,

A^{N}(i,j)=\begin{cases}N^{-1}\theta_{ij},\ \text{if}\ j\in\cal{P}_{+},\\
-N^{-1}\theta_{ij},\ \text{if}\ j\in\cal{P}_{-}.\end{cases}

One can check that
\max\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1},|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}\}\leq 1
, so that (
72
) implies that
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}\leq 1
for all
r\in(1,\infty)
as well.

F.2.1
Notation

In what follows, for
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, we denote
L^{N,ab}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
and
C^{N,ab}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot(A^{N})^{\intercal}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
, where
(A^{N})^{\intercal}
denotes the transpose of the matrix
A^{N}
.
On the one hand, both vectors have null coordinates outside of the set
\mathcal{P}_{a}
.
On the other hand, each coordinate
i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
of the random vector
L^{N,ab}
(resp.
C^{N,ab}
) is obtained by summing the entries in
{\cal{P}}_{b}
of the
i
-th row (resp. column) of the matrix
A^{N}
.
Alternatively, the random vectors
L^{N,ab}
and
C^{N,ab}
can be defined as follows:

L^{N,ab}_{i}=\begin{cases}\sum_{j\in{\cal{P}}_{b}}A^{N}(i,j),\ \text{if}\ i\in{\cal P}_{a},\\
0,\ \text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(73)

and

C^{N,ab}_{i}=\begin{cases}\sum_{j\in{\cal{P}}_{b}}A^{N}(j,i),\ \text{if}\ i\in{\cal P}_{a},\\
0,\ \text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

(74)

For
a\in\{-,+\}
, we denote
L^{N,a\bullet}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}1_{N}
and
C^{N,a\bullet}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot(A^{N})^{\intercal}1_{N}
. Note that the coordinates not belonging to
\mathcal{P}_{a}
of these two vectors are also
0
.
Besides, each coordinate
i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
of the random vector
L^{N,a\bullet}
(resp.
C^{N,a\bullet}
) is given by the sum over all entries of the
i
-th row (resp. column) of the matrix
A^{N}
.
One can easily check that

L^{N,a\bullet}=L^{N,a+}+L^{N,a-}\ \text{and}\ C^{N,a\bullet}=C^{N,a+}+C^{N,a-},

(75)

so that the vectors
L^{N,a\bullet}
and
C^{N,a\bullet}
could be defined alternatively through these identities.

For
b\in\{-,+\}
, we denote
L^{N,\bullet b}=A^{N}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
and
C^{N,\bullet b}=(A^{N})^{\intercal}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
. Observe that each coordinate
1\leq i\leq N
of the random vector
L^{N,\bullet b}
(resp.
C^{N,\bullet b}
) is given by the sum over the entries in
\mathcal{P}_{b}
of the
i
-th row (resp. column) of the matrix
A^{N}
.
One can also verify that

L^{N,\bullet b}=L^{N,+b}+L^{N,-b}\ \text{and}\ C^{N,\bullet b}=C^{N,+b}+C^{N,-b}.

(76)

In the sequel, for each
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, let

\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}=L^{N,ab}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{L^{N,ab}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\ \text{and}\ \widetilde{C}^{N,ab}=C^{N,ab}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{C^{N,ab}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}.

(77)

The vectors
\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}
and
\widetilde{C}^{N,ab}

can be thought as the population-wise centered versions of the vectors
L^{N,ab}
and
C^{N,ab}
respectively, in the sense that
\overline{\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}}=\overline{\widetilde{C}^{N,ab}}=0.
Observe that these vectors are well-defined for all
N\geq 1
such that
r^{N}_{a}>0
.
In the next result, we collect some bounds which will be used throughout the section.

F.2.2
Convergence rates

In what follows, for
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, we write
\delta_{ab}
to denote the Kronecker delta between
a
and
b
.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
a,b,a_{1},b_{1}\in\{-,+\}
. The following inequalities hold for all
N\geq 1
such that
r^{N}_{-}\wedge r^{N}_{+}>0,

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,ab},\widetilde{L}^{N,a_{1}b_{1}}\right>-\delta_{aa_{1}}\delta_{bb_{1}}r_{a}r_{b}p(1-p)\right|^{2}\right]\leq KN^{-1},

(78)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\|A\widetilde{L}^{ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq KN^{-1},

(79)

I\!\!E\left[\max_{a,b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\left|(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{L^{N,ab}}-(bp)\right|^{2}\vee\left|(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{C^{N,ab}}-(ap)\right|^{2}\right\}\right]\\
\leq K\max_{a,b}\left\{(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\right\}N^{-2},\ \text{and}

(80)

\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}\leq\|L^{N,ab}-(bpr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2}\ \text{and}\ \|\widetilde{C}^{N,ab}\|_{2}\leq\|C^{N,ab}-(apr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2},

(81)

where
K
is some universal constant. Moreover, for each
\alpha\geq 1
, there exists a constant
K_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that, for all
N\geq 1
satisfying
r^{N}_{-}\wedge r^{N}_{+}>0
,

I\!\!E\left[\max_{a,b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|L^{N,ab}-(bpr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\vee\|C^{N,ab}-(apr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right\}\right]\\
\leq K_{\alpha}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\left(r^{N}_{a}/r^{N}_{b}\right)^{\alpha/2}\right\}.

(82)

The proof of Lemma
F.1
is postponed to Section
F.4
.

Remark F.2
.

Let
a,b\in\{-,+\}
. Because of the relations (
75
) and (
76
), some statements of Lemma
F.1
admit immediate corollaries. For instance,

•

C^{N,\bullet b}=C^{N,+b}+C^{N,-b}
and
\widetilde{C}^{N,\bullet b}=\widetilde{C}^{N,+b}+\widetilde{C}^{N,-b}
are orthogonal sums, and in particular one can deduce from Equations (
81
) and (
82
) that

I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{C}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right]\leq I\!\!E\left[\|C^{N,\bullet b}-pr^{N}_{b}(1_{{\cal{P}}_{+}}-1_{{\cal{P}}_{-}})\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right]\leq 2K_{\alpha}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\left(r^{N}_{a}/r^{N}_{b}\right)^{\alpha/2}\right\},

•

or,
L^{N,a\bullet}=L^{N,a+}+L^{N,a-}
and
\widetilde{L}^{N,a\bullet}=\widetilde{L}^{N,a+}+\widetilde{L}^{N,a-},
and in particular one can deduce from Equations (
81
) and (
82
) that

\|\widetilde{L}^{N,a\bullet}\|_{2}\leq\|L^{N,a+}-pr^{N}_{+}1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2}+\|L^{N,a-}+pr^{N}_{-}1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2},

and

I\!\!E\left[\|L^{N,a\bullet}-p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right]\leq K^{\prime}_{\alpha}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\left(r^{N}_{a}/r^{N}_{b}\right)^{\alpha/2}\right\},

for some
K^{\prime}_{\alpha}
which may be different from
K_{\alpha}
.

F.3
Results regarding the inverse matrix

Recall that
\lambda>0
. Under this condition, the random matrix
Q^{N}:=\left(I-(1-\lambda)A^{N}\right)^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(1-\lambda)^{n}(A^{N})^{n}
is well-defined and satisfies
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}\leq\lambda^{-1}
for all
r\in[1,\infty].
For later use, let us observe that the easy-to-check properties
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(Q^{N})^{\intercal}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}
and
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(Q^{N})^{\intercal}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1}
combined with inequality (
72
) imply that
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(Q^{N})^{\intercal}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r}\leq\lambda^{-1}
for any
r\in[1,\infty].

As suggested by the heuristics presented in Section
2
, a crucial ingredient in our analysis is to study both the sum of rows and columns of the matrix
Q^{N}
. By definition of the matrix
Q^{N}
itself, these quantities are related to the corresponding counterparts computed from the matrix
A^{N}
.

F.3.1
Notation

Similarly as above, for
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, let us denote
\ell^{N,ab}
(resp.
c^{N,ab}
) the random vector obtained by summing, for each row (resp. column) in
{\cal{P}}_{a}
of the random matrix
Q^{N}
, the entries in
{\cal{P}}_{b}
. Also, we denote
\ell^{N,a\bullet}=\ell^{N,a+}+\ell^{N,a-}
and
\ell^{N,\bullet b}=\ell^{N,+b}+\ell^{N,-b}
for
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, and define
\ell^{N}=\ell^{N,+\bullet}+\ell^{N,-\bullet}
. Note that
\ell^{N}=Q^{N}1_{N}
, i.e.,
\ell^{N}
corresponds to the random vector obtained by summing the rows of
Q^{N}
. The vectors
c^{N,\bullet a}
,
c^{N,\bullet a}
and
c^{N}
are defined in a similar way. In particular, note that
c^{N}=(Q^{N})^{\intercal}1_{N}
is given as the sum of the columns of
Q^{N}
. Here
(Q^{N})^{\intercal}
denotes the transpose of the matrix
Q^{N}
.
For later use, let us also observe that
\overline{\ell^{N}}=\overline{c^{N}}
.

Like we did for
L
and
C
, we define, for each
a,b\in\{-,+\}

\widetilde{\ell}^{N,ab}=\ell^{N,ab}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\ \text{and}\ \widetilde{c}^{N,ab}=c^{N,ab}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,ab}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}.

(83)

Moreover, we denote
\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a+}+\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a-}
,
\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,+b}+\widetilde{\ell}^{N,-b}
for
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, and
\widetilde{\ell}^{N}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,+\bullet}+\widetilde{\ell}^{N,-\bullet}
.

Here is an immediate result stating that the
\ell
and
c
vectors are uniformly bounded.

Lemma F.3
.

Assume that
1\geq\lambda>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\max_{a,b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|\ell^{N,ab}\|_{\infty},\|c^{N,ab}\|_{\infty}\right\},\|\ell^{N}\|_{\infty},\|c^{N}\|_{\infty}\right\}\leq\lambda^{-1}.\

(84)

To show (
84
), first observe that

\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\max\left\{\max_{a,b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{|\ell^{N,ab}_{i}|,|c^{N,ab}_{i}|\right\},|\ell^{N}_{i}|,|c^{N}_{i}|\right\}\leq\max\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1},|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}\},

and then use the fact that
\max\{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{1},|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Q^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{\infty}\}\leq\lambda^{-1}
.
∎

F.3.2
Convergence rates

Recall that
\delta_{ab}
denotes the Kronecker delta between
a
and
b
.

Lemma F.4
.

Assume that
1\geq\lambda>0
. There exists a constant
K>0
which depends on
\lambda
such that for all
N\geq 1
and
a,b\in\{-,+\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\Big|\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}-\frac{r_{a}\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\Big|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}},

(85)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\Big|\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}-r_{a}\frac{\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\Big|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}},

(86)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{4}\right]\leq K\text{ and }I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{c}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{4}\right]\leq K,

(87)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{(1-\lambda)^{2}p(1-p)\left[r_{b}+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(2b+(1-\lambda)p)\right]}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{2}}\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}},

(88)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{(1-\lambda)^{2}p(1-p)}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{2}}\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}}.

(89)

Remark F.5
.

First, notice that Remark
F.2
also applies to Lemma
F.4
(with
L
and
C
replaced by
\ell
and
c
).

For later use, let us observe that an immediate consequence of Equation (
85
) is that there exists a constant
K>0
such that
N\geq 1
,

I\!\!E\left[\left|\overline{\ell^{N}}-\frac{1}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

(90)

The proof of Lemma
F.4
is postponed to Section
F.5
.

The following lemma gives a way to control the
l^{2}
norms of the fully centered vectors by the population wise centered vectors.

Lemma F.6
.

Assume that
1\geq\lambda>0
. There exists a constant
K>0
which depends on
\lambda
such that for all
N\geq 1
such that
r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|\left\|\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{N},

(91)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|\langle\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N},(1-\lambda)^{2}(Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}})\rangle-\langle\widetilde{\ell}^{N},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\rangle\right|\right]\leq KN^{-1},

(92)

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|(1-\lambda)\|QL^{N,\bullet}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\|^{2}_{2}\right|\right]\leq KN^{-1}.

(93)

The proof of Lemma
F.6
is postponed to Section
F.6
.

F.4
Proof of Lemma
F.1

Before the proof, we recall some classical results in the three lemmas below.

Lemma F.7
.

Consider i.i.d. random variables
(B_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}
distributed as
\text{Ber}(p)
with
p\in[0,1]
and let
\bar{B}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}B_{i}
. Then, for each
\alpha\geq 1
there exists a constant
C_{\alpha}>0
depending only on
\alpha
such that

I\!\!E\left[|\bar{B}-p|^{\alpha}\right]\leq C_{\alpha}N^{-\alpha/2}.

Hoeffding’s inequality implies that for any
x>0
,

I\!\!P\left(|\bar{B}-p|\geq x\right)\leq 2e^{-2x^{2}N}.

Now, using the tail sum formula for the expectation one can show that

I\!\!E\left[|\bar{B}-p|^{\alpha}\right]\leq 2\alpha\int_{0}^{\infty}x^{\alpha-1}e^{-2x^{2}N}dx=\alpha\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{N}}I\!\!E\left[Z^{\alpha-1}1_{Z>0}\right],

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,(4N)^{-1})
. Finally, by observing that
Y=(4N)^{1/2}Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, it follows that

\alpha\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{N}}I\!\!E\left[Z^{\alpha-1}1_{Z>0}\right]=\alpha\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}}{2^{\alpha-1}}N^{-\alpha/2}I\!\!E\left[Y^{\alpha-1}1_{Y>0}\right],

and the result follows.
∎

Lemma F.8
.

Let
(B_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
be i.i.d. random variables distributed as
\text{Ber}(p)
with
p\in[0,1]
. For
U,V\subset[N]
and
\epsilon\in\{-1,1\}
, define
W_{i}=\epsilon N^{-1}\sum_{j\in V}B_{ij}
for
i\in U
, and
W_{i}=0
, for
i\in U^{c}
. Denote
W=(W_{1},\ldots,W_{N})
. Then, for any
\alpha\geq 1
, there exists a constant
K_{\alpha}>0
depending only on
\alpha
such that

I\!\!E\left(\|W-p\epsilon|V|N^{-1}1_{U}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right)\leq K_{\alpha}\left(\frac{|U|}{|V|}\right)^{\alpha/2}.

(94)

Jensen inequality combined with Lemma
F.7
and the fact that
|V|/N\leq 1
implies that for any
\alpha\geq 1
,

I\!\!E\left[\|W-p\epsilon|V|N^{-1}1_{U}\|^{2\alpha}_{2}\right]\leq|U|^{\alpha}\left(|V|N^{-1}\right)^{2\alpha}(C_{2\alpha}|V|^{-(2\alpha)/2})\leq C_{2\alpha}\left(\frac{|U|}{|V|}\right)^{\alpha},

where the constant
C_{2\alpha}
is the one of Lemma
F.7
. By using Jensen inequality once more, we deduce that

I\!\!E\left[\|W-p\epsilon|V|N^{-1}1_{U}\|^{\alpha}_{2}\right]\leq\left(I\!\!E\left[\|W-p\epsilon|V|N^{-1}1_{U}\|^{2\alpha}_{2}\right]\right)^{1/2},

and the result follows, putting
K_{\alpha}:=C_{2\alpha}^{1/2}.

∎

Lemma F.9
.

Consider a vector
v=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
supported on
S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}
. For any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
, the following inequality holds:

\left\|v-\frac{N}{|S|}\bar{v}1_{S}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|v-\xi 1_{S}\right\|_{2},

(95)

where
\bar{v}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in S}v_{i}
.

Consider
U\sim\text{Unif}(\{v_{i}:i\in S\})
, and observe that
I\!\!E[U]=\bar{v}N|S|^{-1}
and

\var(U)=|S|^{-1}\|v-N|S|^{-1}\bar{v}1_{S}\|_{2}
. Since
\var(U)=\inf_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}}I\!\!E\left[(U-\xi)^{2}\right]
, it follows that for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,

|S|^{-1}\left\|v-\frac{N}{|S|}\bar{v}1_{S}\right\|_{2}\leq I\!\!E\left[(U-\xi)^{2}\right]=|S|^{-1}\left\|v-\xi 1_{S}\right\|_{2},

implying the result.
∎

Inequalities (
81
) and (
82
) follow from Lemmas
F.9
and
F.8
respectively. Next, we prove Inequality (
80
).
First, note that
(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{L^{N,ab}}-(bp)=b(|\mathcal{P}_{a}||\mathcal{P}_{b}|)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{b}}(\theta_{ij}-p),
so that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left|(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{L^{N,ab}}-(bp)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\var\left((|\mathcal{P}_{a}||\mathcal{P}_{b}|)^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{b}}\theta_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=p(1-p)(|\mathcal{P}_{a}||\mathcal{P}_{b}|)^{-1}=N^{-2}p(1-p)(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}.

(96)

Since
p(1-p)\leq 1/4
, we conclude that

I\!\!E\left[\left|(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{L^{N,ab}}-(bp)\right|^{2}\right]\leq(N^{-2}/4)\max_{c,d}\{(r^{N}_{c}r^{N}_{d})^{-1}\}.

Similar arguments can be applied to show that

I\!\!E\left[\left|(r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b})^{-1}\overline{C^{N,ab}}-(ap)\right|^{2}\right]\leq(N^{-2}/4)\max_{c,d}\{(r^{N}_{c}r^{N}_{d})^{-1}\}.

Inequality (
80
) follows then from the above inequalities and from the fact that
x\vee y\leq x+y
for any
x,y\geq 0
.

We now establish Inequality (
79
). To that end, first observe that

I\!\!E\left[\|A^{N}\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=NI\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}A^{N}(1,j)\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}_{j}\right)^{2}\right].

Next, observe that if we denote
Z_{j}=L^{N,ab}_{j}-(pb)r^{N}_{b}
for
j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
, then we can write (recall that
A^{N}(i,j)=bN^{-1}\theta_{ij}
for
1\leq i\leq N
and
j\in\mathcal{P}_{b}
),

\left[\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}A^{N}(1,j)\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}_{j}\right]^{2}=N^{-2}\left[\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}-\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}\right)\left(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}Z_{k}\right)\right]^{2},

so that by applying Jensen’s inequality we obtain that

I\!\!E\left[\|A^{N}\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\\
2N^{-1}\left[I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]+I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}\right)^{2}\left(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}Z_{k}\right)^{2}\right]\right].

(97)

By observing that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}\leq|\mathcal{P}_{a}|
and using that
(Z_{k})_{k\in\mathcal{P}_{b}}
are i.i.d. centered random variables, one can check that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}\right)^{2}\left(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}Z_{k}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{2}\var\left(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}Z_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{P}_{a}|\var\left(Z_{1}\right)=r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b}p(1-p)\leq 1/4,

where in the last inequality we have used that
\var\left(Z_{k}\right)=\var\left(L^{N,ab}_{k}\right)=|\mathcal{P}_{b}|p(1-p)/N^{2}
.

Now, note that if
j\neq 1,
then
\theta_{1j}
is independent of
Z_{j}
so that

I\!\!E[\theta_{1j}Z_{j}]=I\!\!E[\theta_{1j}]I\!\!E[Z_{j}]=0
, because
Z_{j}
is centered. Moreover, one can check that
(\theta_{1j}Z_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq 1}
are independent. Hence, by combining these facts with Jensen’s inequality, we can deduce that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[I\!\!E\left[\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq 1}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]+I\!\!E\left[\left(\theta_{11}Z_{1}\right)^{2}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[\var\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq 1}\theta_{1j}Z_{j}\right)+\var\left(Z_{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\left[(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1)p\var(Z_{1})+\var\left(Z_{1}\right)\right]\leq 2r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b}p(1-p)\leq 1/2.

Combining the last three inequalities we show that Inequality (
79
) holds.

It remains to show Inequality (
78
). In the case
a_{1}\neq a
, the two vectors
\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}
and
\widetilde{L}^{N,ab_{1}}
have disjoint supports so that
\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,ab},\widetilde{L}^{N,a_{1}b_{1}}\right>=0
a.s. and (
79
) trivially holds.

From now on, consider that
a_{1}=a
. Let us denote
Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}=\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,ab},\widetilde{L}^{N,ab_{1}}\right>
and prove that there exists a universal constant
K
such that

I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-\delta_{bb_{1}}r_{a}r_{b}p(1-p)\right|^{2}\right]\leq K\left[I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-I\!\!E\left[Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}\right]\right|^{2}\right]+N^{-2}\right].

(98)

First, consider the case
b_{1}\neq b
. Remind that
L^{N,ab}
only depends on
\{\theta_{ij},i\in\mathcal{P}_{a},j\in\mathcal{P}_{b}\}
, and remark that
I\!\!E\left[\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}_{i}\right]=0
for all
i=1,\dots,N
. In particular, the vectors
L^{N,ab}
and
L^{N,ab_{1}}
are independent and

I\!\!E\left[Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}\right]=\left<I\!\!E\left[\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\right],I\!\!E\left[\widetilde{L}^{N,ab_{1}}\right]\right>=0,

so that

I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-\delta_{bb_{1}}r_{a}r_{b}p(1-p)\right|^{2}\right]=I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-I\!\!E\left[Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}\right]\right|^{2}\right].

Now, consider the case
b_{1}=b
. In that case,
Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}=\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}
, and observe that

I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=|\mathcal{P}_{a}|I\!\!E\left[\left(L_{i}^{N,ab}-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}L_{j}^{N,ab}\right)^{2}\right],

for any fixed
i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
.
In the rest of the proof,
i
denotes an arbitrary index in
\mathcal{P}_{a}
.
Next, observe that if we denote
Z_{j}=L^{N,ab}_{j}-(pb)r^{N}_{b}
for
j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
, then we can write

I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=|\mathcal{P}_{a}|I\!\!E\left[\left(Z_{i}(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq i}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right].

Since
(Z_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}
are i.i.d. centered random variables, one can check that

I\!\!E\left[\left(Z_{i}(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}_{a}:j\neq i}Z_{j}\right)^{2}\right]=(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})\var(Z_{i}),

(99)

which, in turn, implies that

\displaystyle I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{P}_{a}|(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})\var(Z_{i})

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{P}_{a}|(1-|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1})\var(L^{N,ab}_{i})=r^{N}_{a}r^{N}_{b}p(1-p)-N^{-1}r^{N}_{b}p(1-p).

Combining the above identity with Jensen’s inequality (and the assumption we made on the sequence of fractions
r_{a}^{N}
), we deduce that Equation (
98
) is satisfied.

Next, notice that
(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1)^{-1}Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}
can be seen as the empirical covariance of the random variables
L^{N,ab}_{i}
and
L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}
,
i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
. In this perspective,
I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-I\!\!E\left[Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}\right]\right|^{2}\right]
corresponds to

(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1)^{2}
times the variance of the empirical covariance of the random variables
L^{N,ab}_{i}
and
L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}
,
i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}
. It is well-known (e.g., see
(
Casella and Berger, 2024
, page 363, exercise 7.45b)
for the case of the empirical variance) that the latter is equal to

|\mathcal{P}_{a}|^{-1}\left(\mu_{2,2}+\frac{\mu_{2,0}\mu_{0,2}-(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-2)\mu_{1,1}^{2}}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1}\right),

where
\mu_{r,s}=I\!\!E\left[\left(L^{N,ab}_{i}-I\!\!E\left[L^{N,ab}_{i}\right]\right)^{r}\left(L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}-I\!\!E\left[L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}\right]\right)^{s}\right]
. We have
\mu_{2,2}=I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab}_{i}-bpr^{N}_{b})^{2}(L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}-b_{1}pr^{N}_{b_{1}})^{2}]\leq(I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab}_{i}-bpr^{N}_{b})^{4}]I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}-b_{1}pr^{N}_{b_{1}})^{4}])^{1/2}
by Cauchy Schwarz inequality,
\mu_{2,0}=I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab}_{i}-bpr^{N}_{b})^{2}]
,
\mu_{0,2}=I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab_{1}}_{i}-b_{1}pr^{N}_{b_{1}})^{2}]
and
\mu_{1,1}=\delta_{bb_{1}}I\!\!E[(L^{N,ab}_{i}-bpr^{N}_{b})^{2}]
by independence.
By applying Lemma
F.7
, we know that, for some universal constant
K
,
\mu_{2,2}\leq KN^{-2}
and
\mu_{2,0}+\mu_{0,2}+\mu_{1,1}\leq KN^{-1}
. Finally,

I\!\!E\left[\left|Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}-I\!\!E\left[Y^{N}_{a,bb_{1}}\right]\right|^{2}\right]\leq K\frac{(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1)^{2}}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|}\left(N^{-2}+\frac{N^{-2}-(|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-2)N^{-2}}{|\mathcal{P}_{a}|-1}\right)\leq KN^{-1},

which implies the result.
∎

F.5
Proof of Lemma
F.4

It suffices to show that the inequalities hold for all
N
sufficiently large. Recall that
r^{N}_{a}=|{\cal{P}}_{a}|/N
, for
a\in\{-,+\}
. Throughout the proof, we assume that
N
is large enough (
N\geq N_{0}
) ensuring that
r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}\geq r_{\rm min}>0
for some
r_{\rm min}
sufficiently small depending only on the choice of
r_{+}
and
r_{-}
.
In what follows, we shall denote
K
a constant which may depend on
\lambda
and which may change from one line to another. The proof is divided in the 12 steps below.

Step 1.

Consider the event

\mathcal{A}_{N}=\bigcap_{a\in\{-,+\}}\bigcap_{b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|L^{N,ab}-(bpr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2}\vee\|C^{N,ab}-(apr^{N}_{b})1_{{\cal{P}}_{a}}\|_{2}\leq N^{1/4}\right\}.

For later use, let us observe that Points (ii) and (iii) of Lemma
F.1
imply that
\mathcal{A}_{N}
is included in the event

\mathcal{G}_{N}=\bigcap_{b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|L^{N,\bullet b}-(bpr^{N}_{b})1_{N}\|_{2}\vee\|L^{N,b\bullet}-p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}\|_{2}\right.\\
\left.\vee\|C^{N,\bullet b}-pr^{N}_{b}(1_{{\cal{P}}_{+}}-1_{{\cal{P}}_{-}})\|_{2}\vee\|C^{N,b\bullet}-(bp)1_{{\cal{P}}_{b}}\|_{2}\leq 2N^{1/4}\right\}.

Combining Point (iv) of Lemma
F.1
with Markov’s inequality, one can show that for any
\alpha\geq 1
, there exits a constant
K_{\alpha}>0
such that

I\!\!P\left(\mathcal{G}_{N}\right)\geq I\!\!P\left(\mathcal{A}_{N}\right)\geq 1-K_{\alpha}N^{-\alpha/4}.

Hence,
\mathcal{A}_{N}
is a large probability event and we will first consider the expectations appearing in Lemma
F.4
under the event
\mathcal{A}_{N}
only. This is used in Step 4.

Step 2.

Let
a,b\in\{-,+\}
. Here, we prove that

\text{(i)}\ \ \widetilde{\ell}^{N,ab}=(1-\lambda)\left[1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}+\sum_{e\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{e})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}+\epsilon^{N,ab}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right],

where

\text{(ii)}\ \ |\epsilon^{N,ab}|\leq(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\|C^{N,\bullet a}-pr_{a}(1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}})\|_{2}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}.

Starting from
\ell^{N,\bullet b}=Q^{N}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}=(I_{N}-(1-\lambda)A^{N})^{-1}1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
, we get
\ell^{N,\bullet b}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}+(1-\lambda)A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b}
. Hence, it implies that
\ell^{N,ab}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot\left(1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}+(1-\lambda)A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b}\right)
and
(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}=\delta_{ab}+(1-\lambda)(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\left<A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b},1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right>
, so that

\widetilde{\ell}^{N,ab}=(1-\lambda)\left[1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b}-(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\left<A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b},1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right>1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right].

Then, using the substitution
\ell^{N,\bullet b}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}+\sum_{e\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{e})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
in the two terms above and the fact that
1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}=L^{N,ae}
, we get point (i) with

\epsilon^{N,ab}=-(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\left<A^{N}\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b},1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right>=-(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\left<\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b},(A^{N})^{\intercal}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right>.

Yet,
(A^{N})^{\intercal}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}=C^{N,\bullet a}
by definition and
\left<\tilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b},1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}\right>=\left<\tilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b},1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}\right>=0
by construction so that

\epsilon^{N,ab}=-(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\left<\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b},C^{N,\bullet a}-pr_{a}(1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}})\right>,

and point (ii) follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Step 3.

Proceeding as in Step 2, one can show that

\text{(i)}\ \ \widetilde{c}^{N,ab}=(1-\lambda)\left[1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot A^{N}\widetilde{c}^{N,\bullet b}+\sum_{e\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{e})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,eb}}\widetilde{C}^{N,ae}+\gamma^{N,ab}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right],

where

\text{(ii)}\ \ |\gamma^{N,ab}|\leq(r^{N}_{a}N)^{-1}\|L^{N,\bullet a}-(apr_{a})1_{N}\|_{2}\|\widetilde{c}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}.

Step 4.

Here, we prove Equation (
87
).

From Step 2, one can sum for
a\in\{-,+\}
to obtain

\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}=(1-\lambda)\left[A^{N}\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\sum_{e\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{e})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\epsilon^{N,ab}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right].

(100)

Then, using the fact that
\|1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\|_{2}=N^{1/2}(r^{N}_{a})^{1/2}\leq N^{1/2}
, we get

(1-\lambda)^{-1}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\leq|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{2}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\\
+4\max_{e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{(r^{N}_{e})^{-1}|\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}|\right\}\max_{a,e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}\|_{2}\right\}+N^{1/2}\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}|\epsilon^{N,ab}|.

Recall that
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A^{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{2}\leq 1
and
|\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}|\leq r^{N}_{e}\lambda^{-1}
(see inequality (
84
)). On the event
\mathcal{A}_{N}\supset\mathcal{G}_{N}
, we have
\|C^{N,\bullet a}-pr^{N}_{a}(1_{{\cal{P}}_{+}}-1_{{\cal{P}}_{-}})\|_{2}\leq 2N^{1/4}
, and using Point (ii) of Step 2, we have

{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}(1-\lambda)^{-1}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\leq\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\left[1+\frac{4}{r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}}N^{-1/4}\right]+4\lambda^{-1}\max_{a,e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}\|_{2}\right\}.

Now, since
r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}\geq r_{\rm min}
, it follows that
1+\frac{4}{r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}}N^{-1/4}\leq 1+4r_{\rm min}^{-1}N^{-1/4}<(1-\lambda)^{-1}
for
N\geq N_{1}
for some
N_{1}
sufficiently large. As a consequence, there exists a constant
K
such that

{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\leq K\max_{a,e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}\|_{2}\right\},

(101)

and Equations (
81
) and
(
82
) of Lemma
F.1
implies that

I\!\!E\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\right]\leq KI\!\!E\left[\max_{a,b\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|\widetilde{L}^{N,ab}\|^{4}_{2}\right\}\right]\leq K,

for all
N\geq N_{1}
. Taking the maximum value of
I\!\!E\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|\tilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\right]
over all
N\in[N_{0},N_{1}]
, we get

I\!\!E\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\right]\leq K,

for all
N\geq N_{0}
. Yet, inequality
84
implies that
|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,ab}_{i}|\leq 2\lambda^{-1}
which in turn implies that
\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\leq 4\lambda^{-2}N^{2}
. Hence, to get rid of the term
{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}
it suffices to write

I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\right]\leq 4\lambda^{-2}N^{2}I\!\!P\left((\mathcal{A}_{N})^{c}\right)+I\!\!E\left[{{\mathbf{1}}}_{\mathcal{A}_{N}}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|^{4}_{2}\right]\leq K,

where we used the last inequality of Step 1 with
\alpha=8
.

Finally, the proof of the inequality
I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{c}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{4}\right]\leq K
follows the same line and is therefore omitted.

Step 5.
Here, we prove Equation (
85
).

We have already used in Step 2 that
\ell^{N,ab}=1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\odot\left(1_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}+(1-\lambda)A^{N}\ell^{N,\bullet b}\right)
Hence, we deduce that

\displaystyle\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}\delta_{ab}+(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}A^{N}(i,j)\ell^{N,\bullet b}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}\delta_{ab}+(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}C_{j}^{N,\bullet a}\ell_{j}^{N,\bullet b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}\delta_{ab}+(1-\lambda)pr^{N}_{a}\left(\overline{\ell^{N,+b}}-\overline{\ell^{N,-b}}\right)+\eta^{N,ab},

where
\eta^{N,ab}=(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\left<C^{N,\bullet a}-pr^{N}_{a}(1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}),\ell^{N,\bullet b}\right>
. In particular, we have

\begin{cases}\overline{\ell^{N,+b}}-\overline{\ell^{N,-b}}=\frac{(br^{N}_{b})}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})}+\eta^{N,+b}-\eta^{N,-b},\\
\overline{\ell^{N,+b}}+\overline{\ell^{N,-b}}=\frac{r^{N}_{b}+2b(1-\lambda)pr^{N}_{+}r^{N}_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})}+(1-\lambda)p(\eta^{N,+b}-\eta^{N,-b})+\eta^{N,+b}+\eta^{N,-b},\end{cases}

and so

\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}=\frac{r^{N}_{a}\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)pr^{N}_{+}r^{N}_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})}+\frac{(1-\lambda)p}{2}(\eta^{N,+b}-\eta^{N,-b})+\eta^{N,ab}.\\

Since by our assumption,
|r^{N}_{+}-r_{+}|+|r^{N}_{-}-r_{-}|\leq KN^{-1}
, we have

\left|\frac{r^{N}_{a}\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)pr^{N}_{+}r^{N}_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r^{N}_{+}-r^{N}_{-})}-\frac{r_{a}\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right|\leq K(|r^{N}_{+}-r_{+}|+|r^{N}_{-}-r_{-}|)\leq KN^{-1},

for some constant
K
that may depend on
\lambda
, and the result will follow once we check that
I\!\!E[(\eta^{N,ab})^{2}]\leq KN^{-2}
.

To this end, we write
\eta^{N,ab}=(1-\lambda)N^{-1}(\eta^{N,ab}_{+}+\eta^{N,ab}_{-})
where, for
e\in\{-,+\}
,
\eta^{N,ab}_{e}=\left<C^{N,ea}-(ep)r^{N}_{a}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}},\ell^{N,eb}\right>
. Remind that
\ell^{N,eb}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,eb}+(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
. Then, Lemma
H.1
can be applied with
V^{N}=C^{N,ea}-(ep)r^{N}_{a}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
,
v^{N}_{1}=0
,
v_{2}^{N}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,eb}
and
v_{3}^{N}=(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
: assumption (i) is satisfied thanks to Equation (
82
) and the fact that
\|C^{N,ea}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, assumption (ii) is satisfied thanks to Equation (
87
), assumption (iii) is satisfied thanks to Equations (
84
) and (
80
) because
\left<V^{N},v_{3}^{N}\right>=(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,eb}}\,\overline{V^{N}}
.

Hence,
I\!\!E[(\eta^{N,ab}_{e})^{2}]\leq K
which in turn implies that
I\!\!E[(\eta^{N,ab})^{2}]\leq KN^{-2}
.

Step 6.
Here we prove Equation (
86
).

Starting from

c^{N}=(Q^{N})^{\intercal}1_{N}=\left(I_{N}-(1-\lambda)\left(A^{N}\right)^{\intercal}\right)^{-1}1_{N},

we deduce that
c^{N,+\bullet}+c^{N,-\bullet}=c^{N}=1_{N}+(1-\lambda)\left(A^{N}\right)^{\intercal}c^{N}
, so that

\displaystyle\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}+(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}\sum_{j=1}^{N}A^{N}(j,i)c_{j}^{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}+(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}L_{j}^{N,\bullet a}c_{j}^{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{N}_{a}+(1-\lambda)(apr^{N}_{a})\overline{c^{N}}+\xi^{N,a},

where
\xi^{N,a}=(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\left<L^{N,\bullet a}-(apr^{N}_{a})1_{N},c^{N}\right>
.
Since
\overline{c^{N}}=\overline{\ell^{N}}
, to conclude the proof of this step it suffices to use Equation (
85
) and to show that
I\!\!E[(\xi^{N,a})^{2}]\leq KN^{-2}
.

Proceeding as before, we can write
\xi^{N,a}=(1-\lambda)N^{-1}(\xi^{N,a}_{+}+\xi^{N,a}_{-})
where, for
e\in\{-,+\}
,
\xi^{N,a}_{e}=\left<L^{N,ea}-(ap)r^{N}_{a}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}},c^{N,e\bullet}\right>
. Remind that
c^{N,eb}=\widetilde{c}^{N,eb}+(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,eb}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
. Then, Lemma
H.1
can be applied with
V^{N}=L^{N,ea}-(ap)r^{N}_{a}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
,
v^{N}_{1}=0
,
v_{2}^{N}=\widetilde{c}^{N,e\bullet}
and
v_{3}^{N}=(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,e\bullet}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{e}}
: assumption (i) is satisfied thanks to Equation (
82
) and the fact that
\|L^{N,ea}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, assumption (ii) is satisfied thanks to Equation (
87
), assumption (iii) is satisfied thanks to Equations (
84
) and (
80
) because
\left<V^{N},v_{3}^{N}\right>=(r_{e}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,e\bullet}}\,\overline{V^{N}}
.

Hence,
I\!\!E[(\xi^{N,a}_{e})^{2}]\leq K
which in turn implies that
I\!\!E[(\xi^{N,a})^{2}]\leq KN^{-2}
.

Step 7.
Here, we prove that
I\!\!E\left[B^{N,\bullet b}\right]\leq KN^{-1/2}
where

B^{N,\bullet b}=\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}-\|(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}^{2}\right|.

Denoting
y^{N,\bullet b}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}-(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}
, one can use Equation (
100
) to get

y^{N,\bullet b}=(1-\lambda)\left[A^{N}y^{N,\bullet b}+(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}(A^{N}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a})+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\epsilon^{N,ab}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\right].

Using the same kind of arguments as in the beginning of Step 4 (except the use of the event
\mathcal{A}_{N}
), we have

((1-\lambda)^{-1}-1)\|y^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\leq 4(1-\lambda)\lambda^{-1}\max_{a,e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|A^{N}\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}\|_{2}\right\}\\
+\frac{2}{r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}}N^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\|C^{N,\bullet a}-pr_{a}(1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}})\|_{2}.

Furthermore, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Step 4 of the current Lemma and Equation (
82
) of Lemma
F.1
, we have

I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\|C^{N,\bullet a}-pr_{a}(1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}})\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq K.

Combining the two equations above by convexity of the square function and then Equation (
79
) of Lemma
F.1
, we prove that
y^{N,\bullet b}
is negligible in the sense that

I\!\!E\left[\|y^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq K\left\{I\!\!E\left[\max_{a,e\in\{-,+\}}\left\{\|A^{N}\widetilde{L}^{N,ae}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}\right]+N^{-1}\right\}\leq\frac{K}{N}.

In turn, we can prove that
B^{N,\bullet b}
is negligible. More precisely, we factorize

\displaystyle B^{N,\bullet b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}-\|(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}+\|(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|y^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}\left(\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}+K\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\|\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}\right),

so that, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and then using Step 4 of the current Lemma and Equations (
81
) and
(
82
) of Lemma
F.1
, we get

I\!\!E\left[B^{N,\bullet b}\right]\leq K\sqrt{I\!\!E\left[\|y^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}\right]}\sqrt{I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}+\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}\|\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}^{2}\right]}\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}}.

Step 8.

Using the same arguments as Step 7, one can prove that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|_{2}^{2}-\|(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}^{2}\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}}.

Step 9.

Here we prove, for all
a_{1},a_{2},b\in\{-,+\}
, that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{1}},\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{2}}\right>-\delta_{a_{1}a_{2}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{1}b}}}{r_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{2}b}}}{r_{a_{2}}}r_{a_{1}}p(1-p)\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}},

where
\overline{\ell^{\infty,ab}}:=\frac{r_{a}\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}
is the limit appearing in Equation (
85
).

Since the coordinates of
\ell^{N,ab}
are bounded by inequality (
84
), we know that there exists a constant
K
such that

\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}-\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{1}b}}}{r_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{2}b}}}{r_{a_{2}}}\right|\leq K\left(\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}-\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{1}b}}}{r_{a_{1}}}\right|+\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}-\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{2}b}}}{r_{a_{2}}}\right|\right).

Hence, using Equation (
85
), Cauchy-Schwarz inequality twice and finally Equations (
81
) and
(
82
) of Lemma
F.1
, we have

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}-\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{1}b}}}{r_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{2}b}}}{r_{a_{2}}}\right|\left|\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{1}},\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{2}}\right>\right|\right]\\
\leq\frac{K}{N}I\!\!E\left[\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{1}},\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{2}}\right>^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\frac{K}{N}\max_{a\in\{-,+\}}I\!\!E\left[\|\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\frac{K}{N}.

Finally, we conclude the step by combining the above equation with Equation (
78
) of Lemma
F.1
.

Step 10.

Using the same arguments as in Step 9, one can prove that, for all
a_{1},a_{2}\in\{-,+\}
,

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}\bullet}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}\bullet}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{1}},\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{2}}\right>-\delta_{a_{1}a_{2}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{1}\bullet}}}{r_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a_{2}\bullet}}}{r_{a_{2}}}r_{a_{1}}p(1-p)\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}},

where
\overline{\ell^{\infty,a\bullet}}:=\overline{\ell^{\infty,a+}}+\overline{\ell^{\infty,a-}}=\frac{r_{a}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}
.

Step 11.

Here we prove Equation (
88
). According to Step 7, the limit of
\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}
is related to the limit of
\|(1-\lambda)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,ab}}\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a}\|_{2}^{2}
which can be expanded as

(1-\lambda)^{2}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}\in\{-,+\}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{1}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{1}}}\frac{\overline{\ell^{N,a_{2}b}}}{r^{N}_{a_{2}}}\left<\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{1}},\widetilde{L}^{N,\bullet a_{2}}\right>.

Hence, combining Steps 7 and 9, we have

I\!\!E\left[\left|\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet b}\|_{2}^{2}-(1-\lambda)^{2}p(1-p)\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left(\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,ab}}}{r_{a}}\right)^{2}\,r_{a}\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{N}}.

In order to conclude this step, it suffices to simplify

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left(\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,ab}}}{r_{a}}\right)^{2}\,r_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left(\frac{\delta_{ab}+b(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}\,r_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1+b(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{b})\right)^{2}\,r_{b}+\left(b(1-\lambda)pr_{b}\right)^{2}\,(1-r_{b})}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{r_{b}+2b(1-\lambda)pr_{b}(1-r_{b})+(1-\lambda)^{2}p^{2}r_{b}(1-r_{b})}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{r_{b}+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(2b+(1-\lambda)p)}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{2}}.

Step 12.

Following the lines of Step 11 (and using Steps 8 and 10), one can prove Equation (
89
). Let us mention that the final simplification here is

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left(\frac{\overline{\ell^{\infty,a\bullet}}}{r_{a}}\right)^{2}\,r_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}\,r_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{-2}.

F.6
Proof of Lemma
F.6

It suffices to show that these inequalities hold for all
N
sufficiently large. Recall that
r^{N}_{a}=|{\cal{P}}_{a}|/N
, for
a\in\{-,+\}
. As in the previous proofs, in what follows we assume that
N
is large enough (
N\geq N_{0}
) ensuring that
r^{N}_{+}\wedge r^{N}_{-}\geq r_{\rm min}>0
for some
r_{\rm min}
sufficiently small depending only on the choice of
r_{+}
and
r_{-}
.
Also, we shall denote
K
a constant which may depend on
\lambda
and which may change from one line to another. The proof is divided in several steps.

We will first prove (
91
). To see that, we start by observing that

\displaystyle\|\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}(\ell^{N,a\bullet}_{i}-\overline{\ell^{N}})^{2}-(\ell^{N,a\bullet}_{i}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}(\ell^{N,a\bullet}_{i}-\overline{\ell^{N}}),

\displaystyle=N\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r^{N}_{a}\left((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right)^{2},

where in the second equality we have used that
x^{2}-y^{2}=(x-y)(x+y)
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{P}_{a}}(\ell^{N,a\bullet}_{i}-(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}})=0
.
Then, we combine (
85
), (
90
) and (
84
) together with Jensen inequality to obtain that

NI\!\!E\left[\left((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right)^{2}\right]\leq KN\left(I\!\!E\left[\left((r_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\frac{1}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}\right]\right.\\
\left.+I\!\!E\left[|r^{N}_{a}-r_{a}|^{2}\right]+I\!\!E\left[\left(\overline{\ell^{N}}-\frac{1}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}\right]\right)\leq KN^{-1},

so that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\|\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N}\|^{2}_{2}-\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|^{2}_{2}\right|\right]\leq KN^{-1}\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r^{N}_{a}=KN^{-1},

which proves (
91
).

We will now prove (
92
). To that end, first note that

(1-\lambda)(Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}})=\ell^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}1_{N}+r^{N}_{-}1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-r^{N}_{+}1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}.

(102)

Then, note that

\displaystyle\langle\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N},r^{N}_{-}1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}-r^{N}_{+}1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}\rangle

\displaystyle=r^{N}_{-}\langle\ell^{N,+\bullet}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}},1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}}\rangle-r^{N}_{+}\langle\ell^{N,-\bullet}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}},1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}\rangle

\displaystyle=Nr^{N}_{-}\left[\overline{\ell^{N,+\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r^{N}_{+}\right]-Nr^{N}_{+}\left[\overline{\ell^{N,-\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r^{N}_{-}\right].

Next, by using that
\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N}+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}})
and that

\ell^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}1_{N}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}})
,
one can check that

\langle\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N},\ell^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}1_{N}\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\ell}^{N},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\rangle\\
+N\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r^{N}_{a}\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right]\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}\right].

Now, observe that using the fact that

\frac{\delta_{a-}-(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(r_{a})^{-1}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}+ar_{-a}=\frac{r_{-}-2(1-\lambda)pr_{-}r_{+}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})},

we can write

\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right]\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}\right]=-ar_{-a}\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right]+\xi^{N}_{a},

where

\xi^{N}_{a}=\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right]\left[\left((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\frac{(\delta_{a-}-(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}r^{-1}_{-a})}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)\right.\\
\left.+\left(\frac{(r_{-}-2(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-})}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)\right].

Combining (
85
) and (
90
) with Jensen inequality, one can show that
I\!\!E\left[|\xi^{N}_{a}|\right]\leq KN^{-2}
,
so that

N\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r^{N}_{a}\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}\right]\left[(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}\right]=-Nr_{-}\left[\overline{\ell^{N,+\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r_{+}^{N}\right]\\
+Nr_{+}\left[\overline{\ell^{N,-\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r_{-}^{N}\right]+\xi^{N}

where
\xi^{N}=N\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r^{N}_{a}\xi^{N}_{a}
satisfies
I\!\!E\left[|\xi^{N}|\right]\leq KN^{-1}.

Therefore, putting together all previous identities, we deduce that

\langle\ell^{N}-\overline{\ell^{N}}1_{N},(1-\lambda)(Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}})\rangle-\langle\widetilde{\ell}^{N},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\rangle=N(r^{N}_{-}-r_{-})\left[\overline{\ell^{N,+\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r^{N}_{+}\right]\\
N(r_{+}-r^{N}_{+})\left[\overline{\ell^{N,-\bullet}}-\overline{\ell^{N}}r^{N}_{-}\right]+\xi^{N},

and the result follows from (
85
), (
90
) and the assumption that
|r^{N}_{a}-r_{a}|\leq KN^{-1}
.

Hence, it remains to prove only (
93
). Starting from (
102
), one can check that (recall the definition of
\widetilde{\ell}^{N,ab}
given in (
83
))

(1-\lambda)(Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}})=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}+\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}\left[(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}+ar^{N}_{-a}\right],

which together with the fact that
\langle 1_{\mathcal{P}_{+}},1_{\mathcal{P}_{-}}\rangle=\langle 1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\rangle=0
implies that

\displaystyle(1-\lambda)^{2}\|(Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}-\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}})\|^{2}_{2}-\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}=\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}|\mathcal{P}_{a}|\left[(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}+ar^{N}_{-a}\right]^{2}.

Proceeding similarly as in the proof of (
92
), one can show that

\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}|\mathcal{P}_{a}|I\!\!E\left[\left[(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a-}}-\overline{\ell^{N,\bullet-}}+ar^{N}_{-a}\right]^{2}\right]\leq KN^{-1},

concluding the proof of the lemma.

F.7
Proof of Proposition
4.2

We are now able to prove the three inequalities.

First, remind that
\overline{m^{N}}=\mu\overline{\ell^{N}}-(1-\lambda)\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}}
.
Thanks to Remark
F.5
, it suffices to prove that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}}+\frac{pr_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

First, note that

Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}=-pr^{N}_{-}\ell^{N}+Q^{N}(L^{N,\bullet-}+pr^{N}_{-}1_{N}).

Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-pr^{N}_{-}\overline{\ell^{N}}+N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}Q^{N}(i,j)(L_{j}^{N,\bullet-}+pr^{N}_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-pr^{N}_{-}\overline{\ell^{N}}+N^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}(L_{j}^{N,\bullet-}+pr^{N}_{-})c^{N}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-pr^{N}_{-}\overline{\ell^{N}}+(1-\lambda)^{-1}\xi^{N,-},

where
\xi^{N,-}=(1-\lambda)N^{-1}\left<L^{N,\bullet-}+pr^{N}_{-}1_{N},c^{N}\right>
is already defined in Step 6 of the proof of Lemma
F.4
. From there, we know that
I\!\!E\left[|\xi^{N,-}|^{2}\right]\leq KN^{-2}
. As a consequence, it follows that

I\!\!E\left[\Big|\overline{Q^{N}L^{N,\bullet-}}+\frac{pr_{-}}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\Big|^{2}\right]\leq\\
K\left(I\!\!E\left[\Big|\overline{\ell^{N}}-\frac{1}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\Big|^{2}\right]+N^{-2}\right)\leq KN^{-2},

where in the last inequality we used Equation (
90
).

∎

Expanding the scalar product
v_{\infty}^{N}=\left\|m^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}1_{N}\right\|_{2}^{2}
and using Lemma
F.6
, we have

I\!\!E\left[\left|\left\|m^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}1_{N}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left(\mu^{2}\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|_{2}^{2}-2\mu\left<\widetilde{\ell}^{N},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\right>+\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{N}.

Yet, by the polarization identity,
-2\left<\widetilde{\ell}^{N},\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\right>=\left(\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet+}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\|_{2}^{2}\right)
, so that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\left\|m^{N}-\overline{m^{N}}1_{N}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left((\mu^{2}-\mu)\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N}\|_{2}^{2}+\mu\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet+}\|_{2}^{2}+(1-\mu)\|\widetilde{\ell}^{N,\bullet-}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\right|\right]\leq\frac{K}{N},

and we conclude this step thanks to Equations (
88
) and (
89
) as soon as we check the simplification:

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mu^{2}-\mu)+\mu\left[r_{+}+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(2+(1-\lambda)p)\right]

\displaystyle+(1-\mu)\left[r_{-}+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(-2+(1-\lambda)p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{2}+\mu(-1+r_{+}-r_{-})+r_{-}

\displaystyle+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}[\mu(2+(1-\lambda)p+2-(1-\lambda)p)-2+(1-\lambda)p]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{2}-2\mu r_{-}+r_{-}+(1-\lambda)pr_{+}r_{-}(4\mu-2+(1-\lambda)p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mu+(1-\lambda)pr_{-})^{2}+r_{-}(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))(1-2\mu-(1-\lambda)p)

and the identity

\frac{1-2\mu-(1-\lambda)p}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}=1-2m.

∎

The proof is divided in the 5 steps below.

Step 1.
Here we prove that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ell^{N}_{i}(c^{N}_{i})^{2}-\frac{1+4(1-\lambda)^{2}p^{2}r_{-}r_{+}}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{3}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

(103)

Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ell_{i}^{N}(c_{i}^{N})^{2}=\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left<\ell^{N,a\bullet},(c^{N,a\bullet})^{2}\right>
and
(c^{N,a\bullet})^{2}=(\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet})^{2}+2(r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet}+((r^{N}_{a})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}})^{2}1_{\mathcal{P}_{a}}
for each
a\in\{-,+\}
. We argue that Lemma
H.2
can be applied to

\begin{cases}u_{1}^{N}=0,\,u_{2}^{N}=\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet},\,u_{3}^{N}=(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}},\\
u_{3}^{\infty}=(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{-1},\\
v_{1}^{N}=(\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet})^{2},\,v_{2}^{N}=(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet},\,v_{3}^{N}=((r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}})^{2},\\
v_{3}^{\infty}=\left(\frac{\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}.\end{cases}

Indeed, the assumptions are satisfied thanks to Equations (
84
), (
85
), (
87
), the fact that
\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet}
and
\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet}
are centered (for instance
\overline{\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet}}=0
) and the following argument: using the fact that
\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}\leq 1
and the previous step, we have

I\!\!E\left[\left|v_{3}^{N}-v_{3}^{\infty}\right|^{2}\right]\leq KI\!\!E\left[\left|\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}-r_{a}\frac{\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

The conclusion of Lemma
H.2
is

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{1}{N}\left<\ell^{N,a\bullet},(c^{N,a\bullet})^{2}\right>-r_{a}\frac{\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]^{2}}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{3}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

Finally, the conclusion of this step follows from the fact that

\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}r_{a}\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]^{2}=1+4(1-\lambda)^{2}p^{2}r_{-}r_{+}.

(104)

Step 2.
Here we prove that

I\!\!E\left[\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\ell^{N}_{i}c^{N}_{i})^{2}-\frac{1+4(1-\lambda)^{2}p^{2}r_{-}r_{+}}{(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{4}}\right|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K}{N^{2}}.

(105)

Notice that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\ell_{i}^{N}c_{i}^{N})^{2}=\sum_{a\in\{-,+\}}\left<(\ell^{N,a\bullet})^{2},(c^{N,a\bullet})^{2}\right>
. We argue that Lemma
H.2
can be applied to

\begin{cases}u_{1}^{N}=(\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet})^{2},\,u_{2}^{N}=(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}}\widetilde{\ell}^{N,a\bullet},\,u_{3}^{N}=((r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{\ell^{N,a\bullet}})^{2},\\
u_{3}^{\infty}=(1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-}))^{-2},\\
v_{1}^{N}=(\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet})^{2},\,v_{2}^{N}=(r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}}\widetilde{c}^{N,a\bullet},\,v_{3}^{N}=((r_{a}^{N})^{-1}\overline{c^{N,a\bullet}})^{2},\\
v_{3}^{\infty}=\left(\frac{\left[1+(2a)(1-\lambda)p(1-r_{a})\right]}{1-(1-\lambda)p(r_{+}-r_{-})}\right)^{2}.\end{cases}

Indeed, the assumptions are satisfied thanks to Equations (
84
), (
85
), (
87
